Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Covenant of Grace**
	+ God gave the covenant of grace to our first parents in paradise
	+ The covenant was renewed and revived in all ages, including the Old Testament
	+ Circumcision and the Passover were seals of the covenant, as are our sacraments today

• **The Church in the Old Testament**
	+ Spiritual and heavenly things were promised under temporal and earthly blessings
	+ Life everlasting was promised, but under types and figures
	+ Christ was shown in ceremonies, rites, and types, which were often dark and obscure

• **The Church in the New Testament**
	+ Life everlasting is plainly promised without types or figures
	+ Christ is revealed in plain and sensible sacraments
	+ The covenant of grace is sealed by Christ's own blood, not just animal sacrifices

• **Knowledge and Understanding**
	+ In the Old Testament, knowledge was limited to the law
	+ In the New Testament, the law is made manifest, and the gospel is revealed in supernatural knowledge

• **The Spread of the Gospel**
	+ The law was only committed to one nation and people
	+ The gospel is spread and preached to all the world

• **Belief in Christ**
	+ In the Old Testament, believers believed in Christ to come
	+ In the New Testament, believers believe in Christ who has already come and is exhibited

• **The Incarnation of Christ**
	+ The incarnation of Christ is a unique privilege of the New Testament
	+ Old Simeon's experience of seeing Christ in the temple is a example of this privilege

• **Our Duty**
	+ We should strive to go before the Old Testament believers in grace and obedience
	+ We have a better state and enjoy privileges denied to God's ancient servants

• **The Significance of Christ's Incarnation**
	+ Many prophets and kings desired to see Christ, but did not
	+ We are blessed to see Christ in the flesh, and should be grateful for this privilege

• **The Call to Obedience**
	+ We should strive to live up to the privileges we enjoy in the New Testament
	+ We should go before the Old Testament believers in grace and obedience.

• **The Importance of Elevating Our Hearts**

	+ It pollutes our actions and hinders our progress in Christianity

	+ Original sin makes it difficult for us to do good and follow God's will
	+ It causes us to stumble and stagger in our spiritual journey
	+ It makes us faint and unable to go on in the course of godliness

• **The Need to Cast Away Original Sin**
	+ We must cast away the sin that is so fit to compass us about
	+ This means acknowledging and repenting of our original sin
	+ We must seek to overcome its corrupting influence in our lives

• **The Importance of Spiritual Exercises**
	+ Spiritual exercises (such as prayer, meditation, and hearing God's word) are essential for our spiritual growth
	+ They help us to overcome original sin and its corrupting influence
	+ They enable us to walk in the ways of godliness and true religion

• **The Goal of Spiritual Growth**
	+ Our ultimate goal is to glorify God in all things
	+ We must prioritize our relationship with God above all else
	+ We must seek to overcome original sin and its corrupting influence in our lives.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Original Sin Remains After Regeneration**
	+ The Holy Ghost says that original sin compasses the believer about.
	+ This means that even after regeneration, sin remains in the believer.

• **Error of the Church of Rome**
	+ The Church of Rome teaches that original sin is not sin after regeneration.
	+ They compare it to tinder, which is not fire but can easily be set on fire.

• **Sin is Still Sin After Regeneration**
	+ If sin were not properly sin, it would lose its nature and quality.
	+ But sin remains in the believer, even after regeneration, and is ready to hinder them in their walk with God.

• **Sanctification is Imperfect in This Life**
	+ Some people believe that a man can be perfectly sanctified in this life.
	+ But the Holy Ghost exhorts believers to cast off sin, indicating that they still have sin in them.

• **The Apostle's Exhortation**
	+ The author of the epistle, including himself, exhorts believers to cast off sin.
	+ This shows that even the apostle and other Christians had sin in them.

• **No Man is Perfect in This Life**
	+ No man has ever been or will be perfectly sanctified in this life, except for Christ.
	+ We should not marvel at this, but rather marvel at God's gift of any sound grace to us.

• **Christ is Our Whole and Only Righteousness**
	+ If a man were perfectly sanctified in this life, he would be perfectly just and righteous in himself.
	+ But this would mean that Christ is not a whole and alone Savior, but only a means to convey saving grace.

• **Faith is Imperfect in This Life**
	+ Faith, which is necessary for receiving God's grace, is imperfect in this life.
	+ Therefore, all other gifts and graces, such as righteousness and sanctification, are also imperfect in this life.

• **No Man Can Stand Justified by Inherent Justice**
	+ The Church of Rome teaches that a man can be justified by inherent justice or righteousness.
	+ But this is not possible, since sanctification in this life is imperfect.

• **Justification is by Free Grace and Mercy**
	+ Justification is not by inherent justice or righteousness, but by free grace and mercy in Christ.
	+ This is why Christ is our whole and only righteousness, and why we must marvel at God's gift of any sound grace to us.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Nature of Justification**
	+ There are two ways a person can be made good: through the pardon of sins and the infusion of inward righteousness, or through the merit of a person's own works of grace.
	+ The latter view holds that a person stands righteous before God through their own efforts.

• **The Imperfection of Human Works**
	+ Human works of grace are imperfect and cannot justify us before God.
	+ Therefore, they cannot stand as our righteousness on the last day.

• **The Error of Self-Justification**
	+ The doctrine that we can justify ourselves through our own works is erroneous and terrifying.
	+ Who can dare to trust in their own righteousness for salvation?

• **The Acceptance of Sanctification**
	+ God accepts our sanctification, but not as the matter of our justification unto life.
	+ Only the obedience and righteousness of Jesus Christ, accepted by faith, is answerable to the law.

• **The Nature of Original Sin**
	+ Original sin is not a quality or essence, but an ill disposition in a person's nature.
	+ It is a corruption that hinders a person from conforming to God's will and doing good.

• **The Experience of Regenerate Man**
	+ A regenerate man feels the corruption of his nature, which hinders him in practicing good duties.
	+ This corruption causes him to leave undone the good he would have done and to do evil instead.

• **The Experience of David and Paul**
	+ David and Paul both felt the corruption of their nature, which hindered them in their good affections and actions.
	+ They prayed for liberty and freedom from this corruption.

• **The Burden of Original Corruption**
	+ Original corruption is a grievous burden for every child of God.
	+ It hinders a person from doing good and causes them to feel a sense of doubt and lack of love.

• **The Contrast with the Natural Man**
	+ The natural man does not feel the corruption of his nature and does not experience doubt or lack of love.
	+ He feels no pride, hypocrisy, or vainglory.

• **The Importance of Recognizing Original Corruption**
	+ It is essential to recognize the corruption of our nature and its effects on our lives.
	+ This recognition should lead us to pray for liberty and freedom from this corruption.

	+ Scripture is perfect and complete (78)
	+ Scripture is sufficient for all knowledge (58)

• **Sin**
	+ Sin leads to ruin and overthrow of government (137)
	+ Sin is a corruption of the human heart (4089)
	+ Sin is a disorder that must be cast away (2328)
	+ Sin is a judgment that must be faced (7677)

• **Spiritual Life**
	+ Spiritual calling is a gift from God (389)
	+ Spiritual wisdom is necessary for understanding God's will (213)
	+ Spiritual zeal is a mark of true faith (262)
	+ Solitary life can be a means of spiritual growth (389)

• **Faith and Works**
	+ True faith breaks out in good works (392)
	+ Faith without works is dead (392)
	+ Faith is a rare and precious gift (392)
	+ Faith submits to God's will (152)

• **God's Providence**
	+ God's special and particular providence is a reality (394)
	+ God's special revelation is a means of understanding His will (76)
	+ God's sovereignty is evident in all things (394)

• **Church and State**
	+ The true church is a visible institution (3435)
	+ The visible church is a means of God's providence (3435)
	+ The two kingdoms of church and state are distinct (351)

• **Faith and Trials**
	+ Trials of faith are a means of strengthening faith (22527, 301, 369)
	+ Suffering for Christ's sake is a commendation of true faith (375)
	+ Weak faith can be strengthened through trials (180)

• **God's Name and Worship**
	+ Taking God's name in vain is a sin (21718)
	+ Swearing is a means of taking God's name in vain (21718)
	+ Worshiping God in spirit and truth is a means of drawing near to Him (65)

• **Eschatology**
	+ The second heaven is a realm of spiritual reality (159)
	+ The third heaven is a realm of spiritual reality (159, 16365)
	+ The universal flood is a means of God's judgment (48, 76, 82)
